*[synthesis]* The camps optimize different outcomes: Zach optimizes VIEWS (top-of-funnel reach), Bonusfootage/Jessi optimize FOLLOWS and CONVERSION (resonance), Adley optimizes WATCHABILITY (psychology beats topic), and Sean sequences broad→narrow inside a single hook. The corpus's own reconciliation is Zach's funnel: wide-net content up top that funnels to a specific buyer [Zach 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZwjsxiL8m/ "Zach · 2026-04-21 · row 373") — wide for reach, specific for identity and sales.

## 3.3 Positioning: own stances, not just subjects

Zach's positioning system: the personal-brand pyramid stacks niche, positioning, originality, and association [Zach 06-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZoDEZOIlkE/ "Zach · 2026-06-15 · row 158"); positioning is your stance on each subtopic within your niche [Zach 06-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZoDEZOIlkE/ "Zach · 2026-06-15 · row 158"). Decide the hills you're willing to die on, then derive your 10 hot takes [Zach 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaYGVmbqSYb/ "Zach · 2026-07-04 · row 51"); hold a strong opinion on ten topics and give a unique experience in each video [Zach 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcxC2UoYX1/ "Zach · 2026-05-17 · row 234"); keep a running list of the 10 topics in your niche to make videos from [Zach 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYSC8ygoldS/ "Zach · 2026-05-13 · row 259"); his entire strategy fits on one Google doc — 10 trial reels a day, a do's-and-don'ts list, ~15 topics for brand positioning [Zach 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYUjpmBIE4V/ "Zach · 2026-05-14 · row 253"). To grow you must be unique, one of one, standing out from everyone in your niche [Zach 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ5fo23IC5s/ "Zach · 2026-06-22 · row 129"). Jessi: your content won't stand out if it sounds like what everyone else in your niche is saying [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXKoMYhAXgI/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 411"). Sean: an oversaturated market is actually GOOD if you're truly unique — your different perspective stands out more [Sean 05-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYmqqE7zr0V/ "Sean · 2026-05-21 · row 220") · [Sean 04-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXdNOQtjuvj/ "Sean · 2026-04-23 · row 361"); and you only need five content formats to grow, not ten or more [Sean 05-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYgQ_oLTVEu/ "Sean · 2026-05-19 · row 227") — small accounts should double down on the five or fewer formats already working instead of experimenting with ten [Sean 05-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYgQ_oLTVEu/ "Sean · 2026-05-19 · row 227") (Chapter 14). Briar's counter-positioning: if you don't have an established brand, get OFF the talking-head format everyone in your space uses and counter-position against the dominant format [Briar 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYS8IZVBUyA/ "Briar · 2026-05-13 · row 256") (formats in Chapter 10/14). Max's discipline: stop niche-hopping the moment it gets hard — stay with the same problem and fix it until it works [Max 04-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXeogtwCOAR "Max · 2026-04-23 · row 360"). Adley: you'll never build your own brand by just recreating other people's viral videos [Adley 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaiGXRSBaRC "Adley · 2026-07-08 · row 23"); take proven virals and add your own unique mechanism — your separator, the reason someone picks you among thousands [Adley 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaiGXRSBaRC "Adley · 2026-07-08 · row 23") · [Adley 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXcVPcRkfhQ/ "Adley · 2026-04-22 · row 364") (the full copying war is Chapter 5).

## 3.4 The corporate goldmine and other under-mined identities

Jessi's contrarian niche claim: people with corporate 9-to-5 jobs are sitting on a content goldmine precisely because they're relatable [Jessi 04-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXsoqOJEWmW/ "Jessi · 2026-04-29 · row 344"); full-time creators become unrelatable to the 9-to-5 majority — that's the gap corporate creators fill [Jessi 04-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXsoqOJEWmW/ "Jessi · 2026-04-29 · row 344"). The unspoken rules and inside jokes of your industry ARE content [Jessi 04-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXsoqOJEWmW/ "Jessi · 2026-04-29 · row 344"): make videos about what everyone in your industry thinks but nobody says, and the weird norms nobody questions [Jessi 04-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXsoqOJEWmW/ "Jessi · 2026-04-29 · row 344"). Reframe what you already know: you don't have to change what you know to make money online — change how you package it [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXIcJ4sEbEn/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 414"); the internet pays for specific insights, so reframe a broad job title into a specific, curiosity-driving insight [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXIcJ4sEbEn/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 414"); see your knowledge through the eyes of someone who doesn't know it yet [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXIcJ4sEbEn/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 414") — people will buy things you think are too obvious [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXIcJ4sEbEn/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 414"). One warning label from her unfollow data: political content drives away followers who come to social media to check out [Jessi 04-24](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXh2UItkqOc/ "Jessi · 2026-04-24 · row 357").

## 3.5 The profile itself

Layton is the corpus's only voice on the bio, and he's unequivocal: vague bios with no information kill growth because people can't tell who the account is for [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52"); you have only seconds to convince someone to follow, and confusion is the number one thing that stops them [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52"); a good bio makes it instantly clear what a visitor gets by following [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52"). Fix it by stating your goals, your why, who you help, and what problem you solve [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52") — then run his stranger test: if a stranger found your profile today, would it make sense? [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52")

> "When you try to talk to a lot of people you end up talking to no one." — Bonusfootage [Bonusfootage 04-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXWr15qAMZb/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-20 · row 386")

## Do this (all cited above)

- Write your one viewer's dossier: name, wardrobe, lunch spot, reading list [Bonusfootage 04-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXWr15qAMZb/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-20 · row 386").
- List their top 5 pains and 5 desires; every video maps to one [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247").
- Draft your 10-topic / 10-hot-take positioning doc — one page only [Zach 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYUjpmBIE4V/ "Zach · 2026-05-14 · row 253") · [Zach 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcxC2UoYX1/ "Zach · 2026-05-17 · row 234").
- Decide your funnel split: wide-net reach content vs specific buyer content [Zach 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZwjsxiL8m/ "Zach · 2026-04-21 · row 373") · [Bonusfootage 05-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX6wEEIA3VL/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-04 · row 317").
- Run the stranger test on your bio today [Layton 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaXla1ozoXq "Layton · 2026-07-04 · row 52").

# Part II — The Craft

# Chapter 4. Topics & Ideation: What to Talk About

## 4.1 Proven topics: Sean's demand doctrine

Sean Stewart is the corpus's loudest voice for outside-in topic selection. **The single most important thing about a video is its topic** [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112"); your topic matters more than your hook [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192"). You can make a yap video go viral with no fancy editing just by picking topics people already have demand for [Sean 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagUG5kSGsq "Sean · 2026-07-07 · row 27"). The best yappers check what people are actually searching for instead of an idea in their head [Sean 05-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYr21YVzy8l/ "Sean · 2026-05-23 · row 217"); don't brain-dump — talk about what people want to hear [Sean 05-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYV5iwLTl-8/ "Sean · 2026-05-15 · row 246"); your own idea is bad; a proven topic gets more eyeballs [Sean 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY8Auo_SMgP/ "Sean · 2026-05-29 · row 201") · [Sean 05-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY3Iv_vztoR/ "Sean · 2026-05-28 · row 205"). Find a proven/trending topic by watching several creators blow up on it first [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112") · [Sean 06-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZB3HgXTwRg/ "Sean · 2026-06-01 · row 197"); pick "shiny object" topics — little effort, big reward, validated by existing virals [Sean 06-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZB3HgXTwRg/ "Sean · 2026-06-01 · row 197"); swapping a trending topic into a proven format can multiply views — changing "ChatGPT" to "Claude" 3X'd his [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192"); stack a second trending topic inside the same video [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112"); tap emotionally-charged debates with huge passionate fanbases — built-in engagement [Sean 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaeL-OdzEK5 "Sean · 2026-07-07 · row 38"); or share a better alternative tool everyone is overlooking [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112"). Ground it in your buyer too: build content around the specific questions your ideal client actually wants answered [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZHCGI2TDI8/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 191"). Zach agrees on the mechanism: talking about a hot, trending topic (right now, Claude Code) is the easiest way to get views [Zach 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZTGgECB-b/ "Zach · 2026-04-21 · row 377"); reach among non-followers signals a topic has real demand [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac4x9BIKHS/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 44"); once demand is proven, mine additional angles [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac4x9BIKHS/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 44"); and what makes a video go viral is the idea or the topic [Zach 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXb6MeniCh1/ "Zach · 2026-04-22 · row 365").

**⚔️ Split Opinion — Ride proven/trending topics — or make what only you would make?**

**Sean Stewart / Zach — demand first**

A proven topic beats your own untested idea [Sean 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY8Auo_SMgP/ "Sean · 2026-05-29 · row 201") · [Sean 05-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY3Iv_vztoR/ "Sean · 2026-05-28 · row 205") · [Sean 05-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYr21YVzy8l/ "Sean · 2026-05-23 · row 217") · [Sean 05-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYV5iwLTl-8/ "Sean · 2026-05-15 · row 246"); trending topics are the easiest views [Zach 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZTGgECB-b/ "Zach · 2026-04-21 · row 377").

**Max Tornow / Layton / Bonusfootage — self first**

Share what you genuinely want to share and talk openly, instead of copying what's working just to chase views [Max 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadRBfQI9mY "Max · 2026-07-06 · row 42") (Max). The best content is whatever you're feeling right now — real, fresh, authentic [Layton 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaPqbdCxWH4 "Layton · 2026-07-01 · row 86") (Layton). Most people don't lack on-camera confidence; they force ideas they don't love, and that flat energy shows up on camera [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195") (Bonusfootage). A daily idea ritual produces ideas that are true to you, in your voice, that you enjoy saying on camera [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195").

**Briar Cochran — bet on what endures**

The current attention-maximizing "interest media" model will not last; AI will bring back user-customizable algorithms [Briar 06-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZaN3Ijhr30 "Briar · 2026-06-10 · row 177"). People report hating ~70% of their time on attention-optimized platforms — a signal platforms will pivot toward enjoyment [Briar 06-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZaN3Ijhr30 "Briar · 2026-06-10 · row 177"). Bet your content on what endures: education, humor, and (cautiously) inspiration [Briar 06-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZaN3Ijhr30 "Briar · 2026-06-10 · row 177") — and be careful with lifestyle/inspiration content; it breeds jealousy and comparison [Briar 06-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZaN3Ijhr30 "Briar · 2026-06-10 · row 177").

**When each applies**

*[synthesis]* Sean optimizes discovery probability; Max/Layton/Bonusfootage optimize energy and sustainability (flat delivery kills even proven topics — Chapter 9); Briar optimizes platform-shift risk. The corpus's own bridge is Sean's 80/20 (Chapter 5) and Bonusfootage's "naturally contrarian, only what you truly believe" filter [Bonusfootage 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPWiBsA5j4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-12 · row 266").

## 4.2 Ideation systems (never run dry)

**Layton's content tree:** identify your North Star — why you're doing this and who for [Layton 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ4bjx7RIRR "Layton · 2026-06-22 · row 132"); your audience's pain points become your content pillars, and your ideas are your unique solutions to those pains [Layton 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ4bjx7RIRR "Layton · 2026-06-22 · row 132"); once North Star, audience, and pains are mapped, ideas become endless [Layton 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ4bjx7RIRR "Layton · 2026-06-22 · row 132"); the same principles apply whether you're an authority or a total beginner [Layton 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ4bjx7RIRR "Layton · 2026-06-22 · row 132"). His unlock for dry days: everything is content — even having no ideas is itself the content [Layton 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaPqbdCxWH4 "Layton · 2026-07-01 · row 86"); when you feel you have no idea, make THAT the idea [Layton 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaPqbdCxWH4 "Layton · 2026-07-01 · row 86").

**Bonusfootage's ritual stack:** do Morning Pages — stream-of-consciousness writing that clears the funk and calms the editor brain before it sabotages you [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195"); write 10 ideas a day (James Altucher's practice) — the idea muscle atrophies without use, and around the seventh you hit gold [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195"); put your phone on airplane mode and take a 15-minute walk — Stanford found walking boosts creative output 81% [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195"); capture ideas in a voice note while they're buzzing, and when you film, tap back into that activated energy [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195"). His selection filter is the "hmm test": when a keyword makes you go "hmm," that curiosity is the signal to lean in [Bonusfootage 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPWiBsA5j4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-12 · row 266").

**Briar's creativity blueprint:** creativity follows four steps — control your inputs, get silence, then speed or organization, then a data feedback loop [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298"). Control inputs deliberately: Stranger Things won't make you more creative; biographies, founder podcasts, and niche content will [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298"); build a research habit inside your niche [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298"). You need extended silence to ideate — the best ideas surface 30–45 minutes in, after the mind offloads its noise [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298"). To capture and execute you must be dangerously organized or dangerously fast; pick one [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298"). Close the loop with data: track whether your ideas actually perform [Briar 05-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYCl_GlDgap/ "Briar · 2026-05-07 · row 298").

**Zach's harvesting system:** ideation must be deliberate — coming up with viral ideas is the hardest part of content [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"); scroll as a creator harvesting ideas, not as a passive consumer [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"); only engage with content you actually intend to recreate [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"); hit "not interested" on content you can't recreate to train your own feed into a research tool [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"); build a saved library of recreatable videos [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"), categorized by remake effort and viral topic [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320"). Prepare about two topics a day and recycle the same language while riffing [Zach 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaYGVmbqSYb/ "Zach · 2026-07-04 · row 51"). Use AI or search to generate a list of hot takes — discard the ones you hate, keep the one you love [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac4x9BIKHS/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 44"). His "Endless Idea Flywheel" starts with posting volume itself: 10 trial reels a day generates the engagement that reveals what resonates [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac2Vn_IXtw/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 45"); once you spot the pattern, study that subject deeply so you can speak with nuance [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac2Vn_IXtw/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 45").

**Jessi's anti-overwhelm tools:** a bank of repeatable posting prompts/formats removes the "what do I post" problem regardless of niche [Jessi 05-31](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZBF2u4vxuz/ "Jessi · 2026-05-31 · row 198"); series-based content solves daily overwhelm too [Jessi 04-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXp6o2Xkb8-/ "Jessi · 2026-04-28 · row 345") (and gives people a reason to come back — Chapter 14); make the kind of content you personally love to consume — she shared her finances openly because that's what she likes watching [Jessi 05-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYibIyaMZxP/ "Jessi · 2026-05-19 · row 225"). If you don't know what to say: say what others in your field won't [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210") (Chapter 7).

## 4.3 Studying content like a professional (the swipe file)

Bonusfootage's method: build a swipe file — saved collections on Instagram and TikTok for every video you like [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"); don't just hoard the saves; actually do something with them [Bonusfootage 05-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYfAxeYgGic/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-18 · row 230"); once a week, drop the saved videos into a Claude workflow, analyze the transcripts, and store the results in Notion [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"). Studying videos you admire is more fun and takes less time than passive scrolling, because you have a method [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"). When analyzing: study the first three sentences and why they caught your attention [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"); study the second hook, usually ~15 seconds in [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"); identify the framework and why it worked [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"); check the script against the three tests from *Get to the Point* [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"). The purpose is mechanics, not theft: studying others' videos isn't copying transcripts word for word — the more you look, the more you see patterns, sentence structure, flow, and delivery to make your own [Bonusfootage 05-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX1TMLADuX4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-02 · row 326") · [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371") (the full copying doctrine is Chapter 5). Jessi's version of the same practice: pull up an actual viral post, break down why it worked, extract the hook/angle/structure and the communication psychology, then make your own version [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210") · [Jessi 05-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYlSfN8oAHo/ "Jessi · 2026-05-21 · row 222") · [Jessi 06-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaElndexHyX "Jessi · 2026-06-27 · row 100"). Sean's tooling for it lives in Chapter 13 (VidIQ outliers [Sean 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYzj6o1OqW8/ "Sean · 2026-05-26 · row 209")) and Chapter 17 (Claude storytelling bots [Sean 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaO7vcVTcsJ "Sean · 2026-07-01 · row 87") · [Sean 05-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYqP3zCT1NP/ "Sean · 2026-05-22 · row 218")); his competitor-ads variant: open the Facebook Ad Library, sort by most recent, scroll back — ads that have run a long time are probably working, so replicate them; open "see ad details" to copy the exact text and destination links [Sean 04-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXk4jqLEzwJ/ "Sean · 2026-04-26 · row 356") · [Sean 04-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXR3OIAk9EZ/ "Sean · 2026-04-18 · row 394").

## 4.4 Vetting a hot take before you post it

Sean's guardrails for opinion content: follow the 24-hour rule — wait a day and check you still believe it [Sean 06-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZdqM5LTLLv/ "Sean · 2026-06-11 · row 172"); only post an against-the-grain take if you have three examples that justify it [Sean 06-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZdqM5LTLLv/ "Sean · 2026-06-11 · row 172"); test it by asking whether someone would pay $5 for the value in it [Sean 06-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZdqM5LTLLv/ "Sean · 2026-06-11 · row 172"); being controversial just for the sake of controversy is a wasted hot take [Sean 06-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZdqM5LTLLv/ "Sean · 2026-06-11 · row 172"). Bonusfootage's sincerity filter: the angle must be naturally contrarian — something you truly believe — not contrarian to grab attention [Bonusfootage 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPWiBsA5j4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-12 · row 266"); only disagree with what you believe is wrong to your core [Bonusfootage 07-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DapyK6SxuEd/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-07-11 · row 6"). (Zach's bolder "rage bait is fine if genuine" [Zach 05-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYLII2aowx7/ "Zach · 2026-05-10 · row 281") and Sean's own "never engagement-bait" [Sean 06-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZTWAOchBNv/ "Sean · 2026-06-07 · row 182") collide in Chapter 11.)

> "Write 10 ideas a day… the idea muscle atrophies without use, and around the seventh you hit gold." — Bonusfootage [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195")

## Do this (all cited above)

- Map your content tree: North Star → audience pains → pillars [Layton 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ4bjx7RIRR "Layton · 2026-06-22 · row 132").
- Install one daily ritual: morning pages, 10 ideas, or an airplane-mode walk [Bonusfootage 06-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZED1x1szve/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-02 · row 195").
- Convert your feed into a research tool: save recreatables, "not interested" everything else [Zach 05-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX4YP0yoEWj/ "Zach · 2026-05-03 · row 320").
- Weekly: run your swipe file through the transcript-analysis workflow [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371").
- Before posting a hot take: 24 hours, 3 examples, $5 test [Sean 06-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZdqM5LTLLv/ "Sean · 2026-06-11 · row 172").
- Validate topic demand (several creators blowing up on it) before investing [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112") · [Sean 06-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZB3HgXTwRg/ "Sean · 2026-06-01 · row 197").

## 4.5 The Shock Score Kallaway insert

Callaway’s method for picking an *angle* gives Chapter 4 a dial. First decide the angle’s job: **shocking** (for entertainment) or **useful** (for education). Kallaway ▶ Then score the shock: out of a hundred people in a room, how many already believed the thing you’re about to reveal? The fewer, the higher the shock — because storytelling is finding what most people *don’t* believe but will believe fast once you say it. That gap is contrast, and contrast is what keeps them watching. Kallaway ▶

Formula — shock score (0–100)

**SHOCK** = 100 − ( % who already believed it )

Prince Charming walking in the woods scores 0 (everyone expects it). A dragon bursting out scores ~90. Then give them something they *do* expect (they fight), then break it again — zigzagging belief against reveal down the whole script.

**Cross-source:** pairs with Sean’s hot-take vetting (§4.4) and feeds straight into the contrast mechanics of Chapter 6.

# Chapter 5. The Copying Spectrum: Originality vs. Modeling

No question divides the corpus like this one. Every creator has a position on using other people's proven content, and the positions genuinely contradict — from "steal the hook" to "never steal hooks," stated in those words. This chapter lays the whole spectrum out, position by position, then maps where they overlap. *[synthesis — structure only; every position below is the creator's own]*

## 5.1 Where everyone agrees: verbatim copying is out

The one consensus point: **never copy word-for-word.** Sean: never copy a script word-for-word — recreate it with your own story and experiences [Sean 04-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXvNGjBzR5n/ "Sean · 2026-04-30 · row 338"); if you recreate a script verbatim, you're just a copycat [Sean 04-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXvNGjBzR5n/ "Sean · 2026-04-30 · row 338"); use the template but insert your own experiences [Sean 05-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYqP3zCT1NP/ "Sean · 2026-05-22 · row 218"). Bonusfootage: never film a borrowed or AI script word for word; take the structure and drop your own lived experience in [Bonusfootage 05-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYfAxeYgGic/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-18 · row 230"); studying others' videos isn't copying transcripts [Bonusfootage 05-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX1TMLADuX4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-02 · row 326") · [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371"). Zach: audiences see right through copycats [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPQn3vIjfC/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 267"); you can't piggyback off someone else's content and expect to build a personal brand [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac2Vn_IXtw/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 45"). Adley: you'll never build your own brand by just recreating other people's viral videos [Adley 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaiGXRSBaRC "Adley · 2026-07-08 · row 23"). Briar: copying other creators is a cop-out for not developing a skillset [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"). Jessi: copy the principle, not the surface [Jessi 06-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaElndexHyX "Jessi · 2026-06-27 · row 100"). Sean's paradox seals it: the biggest creators are the ones others copy precisely because they understand the emotional mechanics themselves [Sean 05-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY0z05tzaBl/ "Sean · 2026-05-27 · row 208").

## 5.2 The war over everything short of verbatim

**⚔️ Split Opinion — Should you steal hooks and model proven videos?**

**Sean Stewart — steal systematically (the modeling engine)**

The core viral loop: find a viral video from a small creator, steal the hook, add a proven topic, add a unique opinion [Sean 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY8Auo_SMgP/ "Sean · 2026-05-29 · row 201"). The easiest way to find proven hooks is outlier videos from creators under 100k followers [Sean 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYRGznNz_Yo/ "Sean · 2026-05-13 · row 261"); open with a proven opening line borrowed from other virals — he used "the rumors are true" [Sean 06-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZB3HgXTwRg/ "Sean · 2026-06-01 · row 197"). Everyone copies each other — find 10 competitors, recreate their 10 most popular videos, and you have 100 proven ideas [Sean 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXc_FG4juAD/ "Sean · 2026-04-22 · row 362"). Run an 80/20 split: 80% researched content modeled on competitors with your own twist [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvbR4wzgWS/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 214"), 20% original trial-reel content to avoid becoming a copycat creator [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvbR4wzgWS/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 214"). His dosage rule: a video has seven components — first sentence, first thing shown, first on-screen text, main premise, your take, sentence pattern, editing [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213"). A copycat takes all seven [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213"); stealing "like an artist" means taking only three and changing the other four [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213"); steal the structure, not the value — don't take their actual substance [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213"). The growth ladder outgrows it: Level 2 "the Thief" steals what works but risks blending in; Level 3 doubles down on your OWN winners; Level 4 studies the psychology BEHIND proven ideas; Level 5 experiments with formats unique to you [Sean 07-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaTpN03Bb6q "Sean · 2026-07-02 · row 72"). Once you master how each line makes a viewer feel, you no longer have to rely on copying [Sean 05-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY0z05tzaBl/ "Sean · 2026-05-27 · row 208").

**Briar Cochran — never steal hooks; steal principles**

Don't steal hooks: a hook must be unique to capture attention, so copying one does the opposite of what you intend [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"). Stop downloading swipe files and commenting "viral" for templates; the world no longer wants to be hooked — understand curiosity instead [Briar 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcyFHThzop "Briar · 2026-05-17 · row 233") · [Briar 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYw41SuRsCd "Briar · 2026-05-25 · row 211"). Build identity-based, principle-driven hooks from marketing psychology [Briar 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYw41SuRsCd "Briar · 2026-05-25 · row 211") · [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92") — his ten hook principles are in Chapter 6. His principle 7 concedes the artist's version: be unique by taking what someone else said and making it your own rather than repeating it word for word [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92").

**Zach (personalbrandtips) — originality is the growth engine**

Original, one-of-one ideas are the ones that go viral [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac2Vn_IXtw/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 45"). The fastest-growing creators with the strongest brands have extremely unique, original ideas — not repackaged frameworks [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPQn3vIjfC/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 267"). Stealing someone else's viral hook does not work; good stolen hooks still flop [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83") — because the hook is how you PRESENT the script, not the words (Chapter 6). Don't copy other people's content [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPQn3vIjfC/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 267"). And yet his research method mines others' audiences: pull popular comments from mega-viral outlier videos and use them as your verbal hook — proven by a hook comment with 500 likes [Zach 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYS8Y2Lo3Kg/ "Zach · 2026-05-13 · row 255") · [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPhVaVo7bU/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 264"). The distinction he draws: harvest the DEMAND signal (what viewers ask), not the creator's own words.

**Adley — model winners, add your unique mechanism**

Find the top 10 most relatable pages in your niche and pull their most viral videos (Sortfeed); aim for 10M+ views [Adley 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagnDGKhiNl "Adley · 2026-07-07 · row 26"). Take proven viral videos and add your own unique mechanism on top — the separator that makes someone pick you [Adley 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaiGXRSBaRC "Adley · 2026-07-08 · row 23"); adapt a viral framework to your brand rather than copying everyone else [Adley 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXcVPcRkfhQ/ "Adley · 2026-04-22 · row 364"). Then self-copy: be creative ONCE, rinse and repeat your own winning format instead of reinventing [Adley 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXcVPcRkfhQ/ "Adley · 2026-04-22 · row 364").

**Jessi Jean / Bonusfootage — extract the principle/mechanics layer**

Study WHY a piece performed and remake your own version — copy the principle, not the surface [Jessi 06-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaElndexHyX "Jessi · 2026-06-27 · row 100") · [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210") · [Jessi 05-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYlSfN8oAHo/ "Jessi · 2026-05-21 · row 222"). Just steal a proven strategy and cherry-pick what fits — she hands over the exact strategy that grew her [Jessi 05-31](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZBF2u4vxuz/ "Jessi · 2026-05-31 · row 198"). Bonusfootage: borrow a proven format from another niche and adapt it into a whole series [Bonusfootage 05-24](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYuAZoykRUa/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-24 · row 215"); steal mechanics, never transcripts [Bonusfootage 05-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYfAxeYgGic/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-18 · row 230") · [Bonusfootage 05-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX1TMLADuX4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-02 · row 326") · [Bonusfootage 04-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXZz_ZDDT0h/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-21 · row 371").

**Max Tornow — don't copy what's working at all**

Share what you genuinely want to share and talk openly, instead of copying what's working just to chase views [Max 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadRBfQI9mY "Max · 2026-07-06 · row 42").

**When each applies**

*[synthesis]* Arrange the positions on one axis of WHAT is borrowed: audience demand signals (Zach's comments, Sean's topics) → structure/mechanics (Bonus, Jessi, Sean's 3-of-7) → literal hooks (Sean yes, Briar/Zach no) → whole videos (only Adley, and only with a unique mechanism added) → verbatim (nobody). The creators' own ladders (Sean's levels [Sean 07-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaTpN03Bb6q "Sean · 2026-07-02 · row 72"), Briar's principles [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92")) both end at the same place: study until you can generate, then stop borrowing.

## 5.3 Copy yourself: the sanctioned repetition

Whatever their position on copying others, the corpus is united on copying YOURSELF. Sean: recreate the exact format of your previous outlier but swap in a new topic [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112"); as soon as a video beats your average, immediately make as many variations as you can [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192"); don't fear repeating a winning video — you feel annoying long before your audience even notices [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192"). Adley: be creative once, then rinse and repeat that format to consistently hit 1M+ views [Adley 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXcVPcRkfhQ/ "Adley · 2026-04-22 · row 364"). Zach: iterate a winner with a different hot take, fashion, format, or script [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV3N_koq-A/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 59"); recycle winners by converting tactical hits into inspirational content and vice versa — an infinite loop [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQMJL0qPDV/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 81"). Briar's mechanical version for trial reels (speed-shift, flip, new text) is in Chapter 11 [Briar 05-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYAUEg-jRnO/ "Briar · 2026-05-06 · row 304"). Formats as self-repetition infrastructure — Sean's 30-formats-per-idea — are Chapter 14 [Sean 04-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXVaF5Djohr/ "Sean · 2026-04-20 · row 388") · [Sean 05-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX1_JTjzfJ5/ "Sean · 2026-05-02 · row 324").

## 5.4 Don't fear being copied

The mirror-image teaching. Bonusfootage: don't fear being copied; it's the internet — they were going to copy you either way [Bonusfootage 07-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DapyK6SxuEd/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-07-11 · row 6"); people can copy your scripts but not your audience relationship [Bonusfootage 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzm3GWJ9TS/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-01 · row 333"). Zach: the original creator holds the advantage — copies (even AI ones) can only exist after you make the original [Zach 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX95EwuonjY/ "Zach · 2026-05-05 · row 312"). Sean: the most-copied creators are the ones who understand the mechanics [Sean 05-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY0z05tzaBl/ "Sean · 2026-05-27 · row 208").

> "Stealing 'like an artist' means taking only three of the components and changing the other four." — Sean Stewart [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213")

## Do this (all cited above)

- Pick your lane on the spectrum consciously — demand signals, mechanics, or full modeling — and pair it with the 20% originality quota [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvbR4wzgWS/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 214").
- If you model: change ≥4 of the 7 components [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213") and add your unique mechanism [Adley 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaiGXRSBaRC "Adley · 2026-07-08 · row 23").
- If you refuse to model: still harvest demand (comments, outlier topics) [Zach 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYS8Y2Lo3Kg/ "Zach · 2026-05-13 · row 255") · [Sean 05-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYLUon8T6kG/ "Sean · 2026-05-10 · row 278").
- Always recycle your own winners — variations, format reruns, value-type flips [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192") · [Adley 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXcVPcRkfhQ/ "Adley · 2026-04-22 · row 364") · [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQMJL0qPDV/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 81").

## 5.5 The Lego-Brick framework Kallaway insert

Where Sean splits a video into seven *components* to control copying dosage (§5.2), Callaway splits it into seven *bricks* to control the whole build. Any winning video in your category can be exploded into these seven, and any new video is a restack of them. Kallaway ▶

1 Topic what it’s about · 2 Angle the interesting take on it · 3 Hook structure visual · text · spoken · 4 Story structure case study, breakdown, listicle, narrative · 5 Visual format split-screen, full, circle, vlog POV · 6 Key visuals A-roll, B-roll, graphics · 7 Audio music & sound effects

Explode (break winners into the 7 bricks) → Grade (which bricks are world-class?) → Hold / Improve (keep the great, fix the weak) → Restack (your own new formation)

Mnemonic — the 7 bricks

**2 WHATs** (topic, angle) + **5 HOWs** (hook, story, format, visuals, audio)

Beginners should hold the HOWs constant from what already works and vary only topic and angle — it collapses the 200-gap (§1.11). Invent new bricks only once the fundamentals are closed. Kallaway ▶

**Cross-source:** generalizes Sean’s “change 3 of 7 components” (§5.2) into a full decompose-and-recombine system, and makes Bonusfootage’s “copy the mechanics, not the transcript” (§5.1) operational.

# Chapter 6. Hooks & the First Seconds

## 6.1 What a hook is for (and the war over how much it matters)

The consensus mechanism: the first seconds exist to stop the scroll and open a gap the viewer must stay to close. Jessi: you must learn hooks — the hook stops the scroll, and if you can't hook, nothing else you say matters [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210"). Adley: a strong hook opens a curiosity gap that makes viewers keep watching to resolve the question — learning things they'd never have watched otherwise [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13"); a great hook compels people to watch something they didn't know they wanted to watch [Adley 05-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DJZmQoApLj6 "Adley · 2025-05-08 · row 461"). Briar: curiosity is the only ingredient that matters in a hook [Briar 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcyFHThzop "Briar · 2026-05-17 · row 233"). Sean: the hook is the lever — the same video went from 4,000 views on hook one to 14,500 on hook two [Sean 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYRGznNz_Yo/ "Sean · 2026-05-13 · row 261"); a single word change in the text hook can produce huge swings [Sean 05-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX75b6yzYO6/ "Sean · 2026-05-04 · row 315"). Zach: the single biggest rule for going viral is leading with a hot take [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagG-QWlJKy "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 29") — open every video with a contrarian belief in the first three seconds [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagG-QWlJKy "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 29").

**⚔️ Split Opinion — How much should you obsess over the hook?**

**Jessi / Briar / Sean — the hook is the gate**

If you can't hook, nothing else matters [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210"). Hook stacking is the difference between 50–60 seconds of watch time and a high skip rate [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"). The headline/opening text is the single most important thing to optimize [Sean 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXc_FG4juAD/ "Sean · 2026-04-22 · row 362").

**Zach (personalbrandtips) — stop obsessing over hooks**

Stop obsessing over hooks; focus on making the whole video valuable [Zach 06-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZfklPOIDI_/ "Zach · 2026-06-13 · row 168"). Full-video retention beats a great hook — if people don't watch the rest, it won't go viral [Zach 06-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZfklPOIDI_/ "Zach · 2026-06-13 · row 168"). A verbal hook that takes six or seven seconds isn't a hook, it's just time into your video [Zach 06-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZfklPOIDI_/ "Zach · 2026-06-13 · row 168"). And the deeper reframe: low views come from weak camera presence, not bad hook research [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83"); the hook is how you PRESENT the script, not the literal words [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83"); the most important thing is the first FRAME, not the hook words [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV9wPfIsdV/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 58").

**Sean Stewart — the after-the-hook gap**

Everyone chases the perfect 3-second hook, but nobody talks about what to do after the hook — which is what actually differentiates results [Sean 05-02](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX1_JTjzfJ5/ "Sean · 2026-05-02 · row 324").

**Bonusfootage — the anti-hook**

A slow, controlled, conversational "anti-hook" opening — the opposite of punchy "get to the point" — may be exactly what audiences crave right now [Bonusfootage 05-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY-tB_DteB9/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-30 · row 199"). You don't have to rush the opening if your first lines are dialed in; word arrangement matters more than pace [Bonusfootage 05-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY-tB_DteB9/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-30 · row 199"). Briar's parallel heresy: the world no longer wants to be hooked — stop chasing and optimizing hooks; understand curiosity instead [Briar 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcyFHThzop "Briar · 2026-05-17 · row 233").

**When each applies**

*[synthesis]* Nobody actually says openings don't matter — the fight is over where the leverage is: the words (Sean, Briar's principles), the delivery and first frame (Zach), or what follows (Sean r324, Zach r168). Read together: the opening earns the next 10 seconds; presence and retention earn the rest.

The corpus also stacks competing "single most important thing" claims worth seeing side by side: topic (Sean [Sean 06-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ_SQgdzVPj "Sean · 2026-06-25 · row 112") · [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192")), text hook (Zach [Zach 04-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXCbRpKCLlg/ "Zach · 2026-04-12 · row 423"), Sean [Sean 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXc_FG4juAD/ "Sean · 2026-04-22 · row 362")), curiosity gap/open loop (Zach [Zach 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzuDVIoREO/ "Zach · 2026-05-01 · row 331")), first frame (Zach [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV9wPfIsdV/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 58")), delivery (Zach [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83"), Jessi [Jessi 05-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX-hwwUso0I/ "Jessi · 2026-05-06 · row 309")), watch time (Adley [Adley 06-16](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZqfY05BI7d/ "Adley · 2026-06-16 · row 155")), skip rate (Zach [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV9wPfIsdV/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 58") · [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83")). Each is argued in its home chapter — treat "most important" as each teacher's chosen lever, not settled fact. *[synthesis]*

## 6.2 Curiosity mechanics: gaps, loops, promises

Open the loop up front: create the promise right away so the viewer immediately wants the payoff [Adley 06-16](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZqfY05BI7d/ "Adley · 2026-06-16 · row 155") (Adley); make the opening text an open loop — don't answer it right away or people won't keep watching [Sean 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXc_FG4juAD/ "Sean · 2026-04-22 · row 362") (Sean); build a curiosity gap into the hook so the viewer NEEDS to keep watching [Adley 05-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DJZmQoApLj6 "Adley · 2025-05-08 · row 461"). Briar's demonstration: give the audience a little taste without the answer, leaving them waiting [Briar 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYcyFHThzop "Briar · 2026-05-17 · row 233"). Zach: text hooks give context and create curiosity gaps — even open loops when written correctly [Zach 05-16](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYZ0aP2I-UP/ "Zach · 2026-05-16 · row 239"); the winning formula is creative, unique videos built around open loops and curiosity gaps [Zach 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzpA-NIke2/ "Zach · 2026-05-01 · row 332"). (Loop maintenance through the video body — re-hooks, sandwiches, withheld payoffs — is Chapter 8.)

## 6.3 Adley's hook doctrine: rewrite until it can't be ignored

Adley teaches hooks as an editing discipline on your own first sentences. The "duh" test: if someone could say "duh" after your first sentence or two, the hook must be rewritten [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13"). A truism hook — an obvious identity statement — fails because it has no emotion and no curiosity gap [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13"). Her rewrite demo: "successful people protect their investments" becomes "this $20 piece of silk could save you over $20,000" [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13"). Raise the stakes so the viewer feels it matters to them [Adley 05-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DJZmQoApLj6 "Adley · 2025-05-08 · row 461"); open with a pattern-interrupt that physically surprises [Adley 05-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/DJZmQoApLj6 "Adley · 2025-05-08 · row 461"). Start right in the action — don't open by verbally explaining setup, stakes, and consequences [Adley 06-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZswwgByull/ "Adley · 2026-06-17 · row 151") · [Adley 06-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZrTkpLp9_8/ "Adley · 2026-06-17 · row 153"); add a visual hook (someone straining/shaking in the opening shot), then cut back to the start of the action [Adley 06-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZswwgByull/ "Adley · 2026-06-17 · row 151") · [Adley 06-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZrTkpLp9_8/ "Adley · 2026-06-17 · row 153"). For product content: don't open on the product — open on the thing they want MORE, then let the product win the visual [Adley 06-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaDthuyxmfb/ "Adley · 2026-06-26 · row 101"); pair a high-value thing everyone wants against a lesser thing only some care about [Adley 06-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaDthuyxmfb/ "Adley · 2026-06-26 · row 101"); make the desirable thing grow bigger on screen while the lesser shrinks — a subconscious message of superiority [Adley 06-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaDthuyxmfb/ "Adley · 2026-06-26 · row 101"). The umbrella doctrine: to win on social media you have to learn how to engineer attention [Adley 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZt3R_Whgk_/ "Adley · 2026-06-18 · row 148").

## 6.4 Briar's hook system: stacks, principles, identity

**The hook stack:** stack three hooks up front in a seamless transition — an actual hook, a pain-point hook, and a dream-outcome hook [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"); then hook, super hook, and potentially re-hook later [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"). A super hook twists the pain point to raise the stakes [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"); a dream-outcome hook paints the appealing result [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"). Demonstrated in full: pattern interrupt → twist the knife → give the outcome [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9"). Hook stacking is the difference between 50–60 seconds of watch time and a high skip rate [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9").

**The ten principles** (build unique hooks; never copy — Chapter 5): 1. open a curiosity loop [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 2. increase the pain of not watching [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 3. increase the benefit of watching [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 4. reverse the risk — get the viewer to commit something [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 5. time compression ("60 years of knowledge in 60 seconds") [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 6. direct call-out of your ideal customer profile [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 7. take what someone said and make it your own [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 8. subtle social proof without being cringe [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 9. external social proof — borrow credibility from well-known people [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92"); 10. include a buzzword/keyword that resonates with your specific audience [Briar 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaMYOzwNTWu "Briar · 2026-06-30 · row 92").

**Identity hooks:** to lift your hook rate (e.g. from 20% to 50%), use identity-based hooks instead of swipe files or made-up lines [Briar 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYw41SuRsCd "Briar · 2026-05-25 · row 211") — hooks that target who the viewer IS ("struggling content creator") [Briar 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYw41SuRsCd "Briar · 2026-05-25 · row 211"); simple marketing psychology beats copying viral-hook templates [Briar 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYw41SuRsCd "Briar · 2026-05-25 · row 211"). Stakes-raising reframe: instead of "here are four tips," open "I could not sleep because these four thoughts were stuck in my head" [Briar 05-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYAeI_okcj5/ "Briar · 2026-05-06 · row 303").

## 6.5 Sean's hook system: super hooks, proven lines, testing

A "super hook" — one line as your SECOND sentence that gives a reason to watch — can double your views [Sean 06-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaJgRWnzKXW "Sean · 2026-06-28 · row 95"); his proven example: "I studied 200 Alex Hormozi videos," which drove 411k views [Sean 06-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaJgRWnzKXW "Sean · 2026-06-28 · row 95"). Make a bold claim believable by immediately backing it with a supporting second sentence [Sean 06-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZB3HgXTwRg/ "Sean · 2026-06-01 · row 197"). STAR opening: say something shocking (S), then a text hook that complements it and drives curiosity (T) [Sean 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX8a_m4TfhP/ "Sean · 2026-05-05 · row 314"). Even the biggest creators' raw-looking videos deliberately open with a shocking statement or text hook [Sean 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYdCY6_yNqG/ "Sean · 2026-05-17 · row 232"). No hook is bad; a text hook is excellent [Sean 05-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY3Iv_vztoR/ "Sean · 2026-05-28 · row 205"). Broad-then-narrow: open broad enough to qualify reach, then narrow to your niche [Sean 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DajaCmPTJPn "Sean · 2026-07-09 · row 15"). And always test: duplicate your draft, change only the hook, upload both as trial reels to double your viral chances [Sean 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYRGznNz_Yo/ "Sean · 2026-05-13 · row 261") — with proven hooks on each version [Sean 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYRGznNz_Yo/ "Sean · 2026-05-13 · row 261") (testing mechanics in Chapters 11 and 13).

## 6.6 Zach's hook practice: hot takes, text stacking, comment farming

Lead the script with a hot take [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagG-QWlJKy "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 29"); take your hot take and use it as the verbal hook, then riff on it [Zach 07-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaYGVmbqSYb/ "Zach · 2026-07-04 · row 51"); a contrarian framing ("automating a business you can't run manually") is itself a viral hook [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dac4x9BIKHS/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 44"). Two stacked text hooks: the main hook above your head plus a parenthetical beneath adding context, a prompt, or a question [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagP0R_qdbR "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 28"). Pack as much value as possible into the first five seconds, talking fast [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPPFsAIQSR/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 269") · [Zach 05-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYfbVQjokpL/ "Zach · 2026-05-18 · row 228") · [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQGiNloOyG/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 83") — short-form is compressing; pack value tight [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV9wPfIsdV/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 58"). Start the video in the middle of your first word [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaV9wPfIsdV/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 58"). You need either shock value or a great hook — with real shock value you don't need crazy hooks [Zach 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzuDVIoREO/ "Zach · 2026-05-01 · row 331"); add mild shock to product ads [Zach 05-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYKPWIbo_xs/ "Zach · 2026-05-10 · row 282"); trolling plus a "wow factor" boosts creative videos [Zach 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzpA-NIke2/ "Zach · 2026-05-01 · row 332"). Comment farming: pull popular comments from mega-viral outlier videos and use them as your verbal hook — a hook that was literally a 500-like comment [Zach 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYS8Y2Lo3Kg/ "Zach · 2026-05-13 · row 255") · [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPhVaVo7bU/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 264"); if the top comment is a gif, have Claude describe it and use the description [Zach 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYS8Y2Lo3Kg/ "Zach · 2026-05-13 · row 255"). For informative content specifically, you must be really good with hooks [Zach 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzuDVIoREO/ "Zach · 2026-05-01 · row 331").

## 6.7 The rest of the hook chorus

Bonusfootage: the best hooks aren't one thing — they're three things hitting at once: the words you say, the text on screen, and the visual scene [Bonusfootage 06-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZxoaOmlI-_ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-19 · row 143"); most people nail only one layer and wonder why the video flopped [Bonusfootage 06-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZxoaOmlI-_ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-19 · row 143"); a hook's angle can be a promise, a reframe, or a threat [Bonusfootage 06-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZxoaOmlI-_ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-19 · row 143"). You can layer four to five standalone hooks into the first 30 seconds [Bonusfootage 05-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY-tB_DteB9/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-30 · row 199"); an "identity call-out" hook makes viewers curious what warrants the reaction [Bonusfootage 05-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY-tB_DteB9/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-30 · row 199"); a "credibility" hook — you'd teach this to your own kids — signals value [Bonusfootage 05-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY-tB_DteB9/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-30 · row 199"). Signal words: framing content as "a list" promises contained structure [Bonusfootage 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY7oH_mtAbk/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-29 · row 202"); "controversial" signals friction and novelty, plus a mild threat the viewer might be challenged [Bonusfootage 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY7oH_mtAbk/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-29 · row 202"). The green-screen open (the "Lauren Dudley effect") hooks, then cuts to raw yap [Bonusfootage 06-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ7epkDOv0u "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-23 · row 123") — tailor the green screen to the exact audience you want [Bonusfootage 06-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ7epkDOv0u "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-23 · row 123"). Understand hook psychology; don't just copy hook templates [Bonusfootage 05-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXzm3GWJ9TS/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-05-01 · row 333"). Jessi: stop opening with "hey guys" — you're talking to ONE person [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210"); an empathetic, specific hook naming the viewer's frustration outperforms a generic list-style hook [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247") · [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431"); and your hook isn't just words — it's how you make the viewer FEEL through cadence [Jessi 05-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX-hwwUso0I/ "Jessi · 2026-05-06 · row 309") (Chapter 9).

> "This $20 piece of silk could save you over $20,000." — Adley's rewrite of a truism hook [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13")

## Do this (all cited above)

- Run the "duh" test on your first two sentences; rewrite truisms into specific, stakes-raising claims [Adley 07-09](https://www.instagram.com/p/DalXaSJP6RV "Adley · 2026-07-09 · row 13").
- Build the three-layer open: spoken line + text hook + visual scene [Bonusfootage 06-19](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZxoaOmlI-_ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-19 · row 143").
- Stack: hook → pain twist → dream outcome in the first seconds [Briar 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DancL9Bx4fa "Briar · 2026-07-10 · row 9").
- Write a super hook as sentence two (proof/effort/credibility line) [Sean 06-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaJgRWnzKXW "Sean · 2026-06-28 · row 95").
- Test two hooks per video via duplicated trial reels; change ONE thing [Sean 05-13](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYRGznNz_Yo/ "Sean · 2026-05-13 · row 261") · [Sean 05-04](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX75b6yzYO6/ "Sean · 2026-05-04 · row 315").
- Keep the text hook up the entire video (mechanics in Ch 8/10) [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DagP0R_qdbR "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 28") · [Zach 05-16](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYZ0aP2I-UP/ "Zach · 2026-05-16 · row 239") · [Zach 05-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYfbVQjokpL/ "Zach · 2026-05-18 · row 228").

## 6.8 The three-part hook, sharpened Kallaway insert

Section 6.7 established the corpus consensus: the best hooks fire three layers at once — the words you say, the on-screen text, and the visual scene (Bonusfootage). Callaway sharpens that triad into a hard operating rule and adds the sequence that runs underneath it.

**Rule 1 — align, or they churn.** The visual, the text, and the spoken line must all point at the same thing. When they don’t, the viewer sees one thing and hears another, can’t reconcile it, and leaves: “confusion leads to churn; the opposite of confusion is comprehension.” Kallaway ▶ And the visual lands first — “speed of light is faster than speed of sound” — so it must stop the scroll on its own before a word is read or heard. Kallaway ▶

Formula — the alignment rule · Visual = Text = Spoken → **comprehension** · any mismatch → confusion → **churn**

**Rule 2 — the sequence: Stun → Context → Contrast.** Kallaway ▶ Drop the viewer mid-story and you break the brain’s top-down read (“the jump was crazy” — whose jump, over what?). Set the context first, *then* open the gap.

Visual STUN (a set or motion the feed doesn’t expect) → Context (sentence one says what it’s about) → Contrast (the gap between expected and shown)

**Rule 3 — direct contrast beats implied, and here’s the geometry.** An *implied* contrast is a bare contrarian line (“cars are driving backwards and nobody knows why”) — it works, but it makes the viewer assemble the missing belief. A *direct* contrast lays the common belief out first (“most people think hooks are X — but the real driver is Y”), which supplies the context and widens the swing. Kallaway ▶

−100 the reveal 0 the setup +100

Implied: 0 → −100 = a 100 swing · Direct: lean them to +100 first, then flip to −100 = a **200** swing. Laying the belief first **doubles the arc**.

**Cross-source:** extends Bonusfootage’s three-layer hook (§6.7), Adley’s “duh test” and pattern-interrupt (§6.3), and Sean’s contrast & curiosity mechanics (§6.5) — adding the alignment-or-churn rule, the Stun → Context → Contrast order, and the arc geometry.

# Chapter 7. Scripting & Storytelling

## 7.1 To script or not to script

**⚔️ Split Opinion — Script your videos — or never script?**

**Jessi Jean — script, especially early**

Script your videos, especially early on — most of her first months were scripted using a teleprompter, sticky notes on her phone, or a notebook [Jessi 06-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ3KbdXPnO4 "Jessi · 2026-06-21 · row 135"). "Yapping" means talking direct to camera by ANY means — scripted, with aids, or off the top of your head [Jessi 06-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ3KbdXPnO4 "Jessi · 2026-06-21 · row 135"). When riffing off the cuff, think in short fragments and avoid filler instead of rambling [Jessi 06-21](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ3KbdXPnO4 "Jessi · 2026-06-21 · row 135"). Pros format and restructure their scripts before going live to tell the story in the most engaging way [Jessi 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYv2MxNJVjk/ "Jessi · 2026-05-25 · row 212").

**Bonusfootage / Sean / Layton — scripted isn't fake**

Unscripted content isn't automatically more real or honest than scripted content [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). Reading from a script can mean you care enough not to waste the viewer's time [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). Rehearsal isn't inauthentic — stand-up comedians do the same bit 200 nights [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). Off the top, outline, or word-for-word: all fine [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"); whichever mode works for you, run with it [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). The real work is the pre-work: the script and message-crafting that makes the video land [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). Sean: it's a lie that authentic content means unscripted [Sean 05-29](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY7I7gTz61U/ "Sean · 2026-05-29 · row 203"); being authentic does not mean being unprepared [Sean 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYdCY6_yNqG/ "Sean · 2026-05-17 · row 232"); spend 80% of your time preparing and 20% making — obsess over every single line before filming [Sean 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZGrs9pzRlx/ "Sean · 2026-06-03 · row 192"). Layton: script your videos so you spend less time in front of camera being watched by onlookers [Layton 11-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DQlfCILEpwP "Layton · 2025-11-03 · row 460").

**Zach (personalbrandtips) — don't script; speak from experience**

Don't script your videos or use a teleprompter — just speak from your unique experience [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPPhPAICj0/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 268"); you don't need a teleprompter or script — speak on what you genuinely know [Zach 05-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYLKD5WoIHQ/ "Zach · 2026-05-10 · row 279"). Never read your script word for word on camera [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaVNKx-Ibci/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 68"). His ladder ranks scripting as the bottom: the lowest level scripts line-by-line and cuts between each line — "that isn't real yapping" [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadfJ_rqJY3/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 41"); beginners (90% of people) write a scripted hook and use a teleprompter [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadfJ_rqJY3/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 41"); certified yappers riff unscripted in one uncut clip [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadfJ_rqJY3/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 41"); the top level goes 100% unscripted with zero cuts [Zach 07-06](https://www.instagram.com/p/DadfJ_rqJY3/ "Zach · 2026-07-06 · row 41"). His compromise workflow: deliver the HOOK word-for-word (you wrote it for a reason), then go off script and ad-lib, alternating; ad-lib the last 15 seconds [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaVNKx-Ibci/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 68"). And yet he also says: don't overthink your content; just have a really good script [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaVXsIzKa9v/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 64") — and teaches script formulas (below).

**Briar Cochran — unscripted one-shot is the format's identity**

A "yapper" is an unscripted, one-shot, authentic creator (phone in a car, on a walk) — the opposite of the scripted talking-head with hyper-edits — and yappers win on volume and authentic connection, not polish [Briar 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYQUGahh49r/ "Briar · 2026-05-12 · row 263"). The only way to become a great yapper is extreme volume: expect ~250 unscripted, unedited pieces before flowing conscious thought on camera [Briar 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYQUGahh49r/ "Briar · 2026-05-12 · row 263").

**When each applies**

*[synthesis]* The corpus converges on structure-always, verbatim-reading-rarely: even the anti-script camp scripts the hook [Zach 07-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaVNKx-Ibci/ "Zach · 2026-07-03 · row 68") and follows formulas [Zach 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dag3VjHIHFB "Zach · 2026-07-08 · row 25") · [Zach 05-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYkRwb9oTTe/ "Zach · 2026-05-20 · row 223"), and even the pro-script camp restructures for engagement rather than reciting [Jessi 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYv2MxNJVjk/ "Jessi · 2026-05-25 · row 212") · [Bonusfootage 06-18](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZu3zezgtr2/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-18 · row 147"). Choose by stage (Jessi: aids early), by format identity (Briar: one-shot yaps), and by prep budget (Sean: 80/20).

## 7.2 Structure formulas (the corpus's script skeletons)

**Zach's formula set.** The "science" of yapping: viral hooks, curiosity gaps, open loops, tactical/inspirational value, negative framing, authority, and social proof [Zach 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dag3VjHIHFB "Zach · 2026-07-08 · row 25"). The perfect yap: negative-framing hook → curiosity gap → storytelling with tactical value → social proof → witty CTA [Zach 07-08](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dag3VjHIHFB "Zach · 2026-07-08 · row 25"). The repeatable three-step loop: hot take → actionable value → open/close loop — repeated three times fills a 35–50 second video [Zach 05-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYkRwb9oTTe/ "Zach · 2026-05-20 · row 223"). Structure content as hook/hot take, then curiosity gap or open loop, then tactical value [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQElE0Iktx/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 85"); pack value tight, remove all filler words, define terms clearly [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQElE0Iktx/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 85"); give people extremely simple ideas they can digest easily [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQElE0Iktx/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 85"). Most content is unscripted, but picked from proven-demand subtopics with a hot take, spoken in hooks and open loops [Zach 05-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYkRwb9oTTe/ "Zach · 2026-05-20 · row 223").

**Sean's STAR method.** Yapping to camera is not brain-dumping — every big creator uses the same repeatable structure [Sean 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX8a_m4TfhP/ "Sean · 2026-05-05 · row 314") · [Sean 05-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYV5iwLTl-8/ "Sean · 2026-05-15 · row 246"): **S**hocking statement → **T**ext hook that complements it → **A**chievement that backs your claim → **R**oadmap: a visual step-by-step to the goal [Sean 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX8a_m4TfhP/ "Sean · 2026-05-05 · row 314") — and give the roadmap a unique name nobody's heard to hold viewers to the very end [Sean 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX8a_m4TfhP/ "Sean · 2026-05-05 · row 314"). Leading with an achievement, stated in the caption too, is a proven core element [Sean 05-17](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYdCY6_yNqG/ "Sean · 2026-05-17 · row 232"). Effective yapping follows a secret structure that keeps people hooked [Sean 05-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYV5iwLTl-8/ "Sean · 2026-05-15 · row 246"); random talking is bad, a researched topic is good, structure is excellent [Sean 05-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY3Iv_vztoR/ "Sean · 2026-05-28 · row 205"); a plain step-by-step is good, but a unique framework is excellent [Sean 05-28](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY3Iv_vztoR/ "Sean · 2026-05-28 · row 205"). Every video has seven components — first sentence, first visual, first on-screen text, premise, your take, sentence pattern, editing [Sean 05-25](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYvjqwyz8L1/ "Sean · 2026-05-25 · row 213").

**Bonusfootage's minimal skeleton.** Stripped down, yapping is you, a hook, a point, maybe a story, and getting people to follow — with nothing to hide behind [Bonusfootage 06-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZ5-sXuACTz "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-22 · row 125").

## 7.3 One topic, one takeaway, deliberately shallow

Zach: every video must have exactly one topic, one takeaway, and one story [Zach 04-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXCbRpKCLlg/ "Zach · 2026-04-12 · row 423"); the biggest beginner mistake is telling too many stories with too many topics in one video [Zach 04-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXCbRpKCLlg/ "Zach · 2026-04-12 · row 423"). Every video should be one of three angles — informative/educational, entertaining, or storytelling — and lean FULLY into one [Zach 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXb6MeniCh1/ "Zach · 2026-04-22 · row 365"); one topic can be executed as any of the three plays [Zach 04-22](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXb6MeniCh1/ "Zach · 2026-04-22 · row 365"). There are only two types of value: tactical (optimizes for saves) and inspirational (optimizes for likes and follows) [Zach 07-10](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaoPPu4IYmX/ "Zach · 2026-07-10 · row 7") · [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQMJL0qPDV/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 81"); decide per video whether you want a save or to inspire [Zach 07-01](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaQMJL0qPDV/ "Zach · 2026-07-01 · row 81"). His depth control is deliberate vagueness: when you make the video, stay on topic and keep it vague — don't go into detail [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPhVaVo7bU/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 264"); make a separate video for anyone who wants the detail [Zach 05-05](https://www.instagram.com/p/DX94N39IWAa/ "Zach · 2026-05-05 · row 313"). Bonusfootage's matching rule from the editing side: when you're just talking on camera, too many details bore viewers — keep detail out of the video [Bonusfootage 04-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXotsTtgI0R/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-27 · row 352") (his caption fix is in Chapter 10). Have one clear takeaway or hot take per video — an opinion people either strongly agree or strongly disagree with [Zach 05-12](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYPhVaVo7bU/ "Zach · 2026-05-12 · row 264").

## 7.4 Talk to ONE person, as "you"

Zach: low views come from addressing too many people at once [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafpTjmI2uS "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 31"); address one person at a time — as if you're FaceTiming them [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafpTjmI2uS "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 31"); rewrite plural/group statements into direct second-person questions [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafpTjmI2uS "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 31"); address the viewer as "you," not "people" — the viewer only cares about themselves [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafoOR_I90d/ "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 32"); be highly conscious of the exact words you use [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafoOR_I90d/ "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 32"); the viewer must feel personally called out [Zach 07-07](https://www.instagram.com/p/DafpTjmI2uS "Zach · 2026-07-07 · row 31"). Jessi: talk TO or WITH your audience, never AT them — talking at people is a top reason content fails to connect [Jessi 05-23](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYsX3akMf16/ "Jessi · 2026-05-23 · row 216"); be less formal — talk like you're talking to your best friend [Jessi 05-26](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYyV-ZNROo1/ "Jessi · 2026-05-26 · row 210"). (Bonusfootage's one-share/one-person doctrine is in Chapter 3 [Bonusfootage 04-20](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXWr15qAMZb/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-20 · row 386").)

## 7.5 Engineering feeling: resonance over information

Jessi's core scripting doctrine: humans do not connect through information; they connect through recognition and emotional resonance [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247"). Most creators talk at people and never learn to articulate what's happening INSIDE someone [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247"). Articulate what's going on in your ideal follower's head that they're too embarrassed to say out loud — in specific detail — and they lock in [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431") · [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXKoMYhAXgI/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 411"); people must feel understood before they'll listen to your tips [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431"); when someone feels deeply understood, they assume you must also know the solution [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431"). If solution content isn't landing, add MORE emotional resonance [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431"); make some videos that ONLY describe how your follower feels, no solution at all [Jessi 04-11](https://www.instagram.com/p/DW-WfiJkdxa/ "Jessi · 2026-04-11 · row 431"); make content that leaves the viewer feeling a little emotionally exposed — like you get them [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247"). Decide the emotion you want the viewer to feel by the end BEFORE making the video [Jessi 04-15](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXKoMYhAXgI/ "Jessi · 2026-04-15 · row 411"). Better content is not always better information [Jessi 05-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DYVhoB-tEGr/ "Jessi · 2026-05-14 · row 247").

Sean's line-level version: every single sentence should make the viewer feel something [Sean 05-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY0z05tzaBl/ "Sean · 2026-05-27 · row 208"); master how each line makes a viewer feel and you no longer need to copy anyone [Sean 05-27](https://www.instagram.com/p/DY0z05tzaBl/ "Sean · 2026-05-27 · row 208"). Adley's reverse design: design the comment section — the exact reactions you want — before you design the video [Adley 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaOeTp6ye-7/ "Adley · 2026-06-30 · row 89"); don't make videos about what you want to say; make them about what you want the viewer to think, feel, say, and do [Adley 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaOeTp6ye-7/ "Adley · 2026-06-30 · row 89"); design in reverse because that mirrors how the viewer experiences it [Adley 06-30](https://www.instagram.com/p/DaOeTp6ye-7/ "Adley · 2026-06-30 · row 89"). Bonusfootage's bar for a point worth making: say something new that's true — specific AND universal — that helps the viewer understand something they didn't before, preferably about themselves [Bonusfootage 04-14](https://www.instagram.com/p/DXH1PgPjRM4/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-04-14 · row 416"); the simplest short-form education framework: make people feel seen, then show them [Bonusfootage 06-03](https://www.instagram.com/p/DZIUcLBN9gR/ "Bonusfootage · 2026-06-03 · row 189").

## 7.6 Storytelling technique

Briar brings the screenwriting tools.
